Well, I'd say it's definitely interfering. I mean... I've had some near-missed shifts lately. And I'm just having a lot more trouble keeping up with the POS reports and inventory at work. I just get this overwhelming feeling that I deserve whatever bad things happen to me because of it. That's actually a big part of why I came in.

But then other nights, I'll come home after my shift and just eat whatever's in the kitchen, just trying to feel settled, I guess. So my weight is about the same, but my habits are definitely off.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I don't have any actual plans to hurt myself, no. But... well, sometimes when I'm lying awake before an early shift, I do think it would just be easier if I didn't wake up. Like, if I didn't have to get out of bed and just keep failing at everything. So I guess I have those kinds of thoughts, but I wouldn't actually do anything to myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, both of those things happen. People at work have pointed it out. Sometimes I get really restless, like I can't stop moving, so I just pace around the restaurant checking the same tables and looking at the same receipts over and over. But then other times, I get so slowed down. Like, just grabbing menus or walking over to the POS system takes me way longer than it should. It's like I'm moving through mud.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much everything. I used to enjoy watching a game on my days off or grabbing a drink with the other servers after our shift. But lately... well, most days there’s just this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. I feel like I'm just going through the motions. Even at work, I'm carrying plates and smiling at tables just because I’m supposed to, not because I actually feel anything good. I just don't get that spark or enjoyment out of things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your depression is mild, or is it interfering with your daily life more significantly?\n\nPatient: Well, I'd say it's definitely interfering. I mean... I've had some near-missed shifts lately. And I'm just having a lot more trouble keeping up with the POS reports and inventory at work. It really makes me worry that I could lose my job, and... well, then I'd lose my condo. I've already made some stupid mistakes with my mortgage paperwork recently because my head just isn't in it. \n\nPlus, I've really pulled back from my brother, and my coworkers too. I just keep to myself. It's gotten to the point where people have actually started noticing and saying that I'm not myself. So yeah, it's definitely affecting my daily life a lot.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by feeling like you've let your family down and that you deserve bad things to happen to you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's just... I took on this mortgage for the condo, and I keep feeling like I've completely let my family down by doing it. Like it was a mistake I can't fix. And then at work, every time I mess up an order or fall behind, it just feels like proof that I'm useless. I just get this overwhelming feeling that I deserve whatever bad things happen to me because of it. That's actually a big part of why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your depression has been present for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I would say so. For most of this whole period, even when some days were a little bit lighter, I've been weighed down by that empty, guilty feeling more often than not. It's just... it's pretty much always there in the background, most of the day, almost every day.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your depression has interfered with your ability to function at work or in other areas of your life for at least two years?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not two years. I'd say... these depressed, guilty periods have been present for about a year and two months of this whole time. So it hasn't been quite two years, but it's still been going on for a while.\n\nDoctor: Has your depression lasted for two years or more?\n\nPatient: No, like I just mentioned, it hasn't been two years. It's been about a year and two months.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But... well, sometimes when I'm lying awake before an early shift, I do think it would just be easier if I didn't wake up. Like, if I didn't have to get out of bed and just keep failing at everything. It's like I'm moving through mud.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much everything. But as far as losing interest in my hobbies or anything, no, I haven't really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a decrease in pleasure when doing things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um, like I just mentioned, no, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I still get the same amount of enjoyment out of things when I'm not at work. It's really just that heavy feeling of guilt I was talking about, but I still enjoy my hobbies just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. Most days there's just this... this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. It's like I'm just going through the motions at work. I'm carrying plates and smiling at the customers because I know I'm supposed to, not because I actually feel anything good. So, yeah, that feeling is there most of the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Well, my weight hasn't really changed that much, but my eating has been... kind of all over the place. Like, some weeks at work I barely even touch the staff meal, I just have no appetite. But then other nights, I'll come home after my shift and just eat whatever's in the kitchen, just trying to feel settled, I guess. So my weight is about the same, but my habits are definitely off.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I don't have any actual plans to hurt myself, no. But... well, sometimes when I'm lying awake before an early shift, I do think it would just be easier if I didn't wake up. Like, if I didn't have to get out of bed and just keep failing at everything. So I guess I have those kinds of thoughts, but I wouldn't actually do anything to myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, both of those things happen. People at work have pointed it out. Sometimes I get really restless, like I can't stop moving, so I just pace around the restaurant checking the same tables and looking at the same receipts over and over. But then other times, I get so slowed down. Like, just grabbing menus or walking over to the POS system takes me way longer than it should. It's like I'm moving through mud.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much everything. I used to enjoy watching a game on my days off or grabbing a drink with the other servers after our shift. But lately... well, most days there’s just this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. I feel like I'm just going through the motions. Even at work, I'm carrying plates and smiling at tables just because I’m supposed to, not because I actually feel anything good. I just don't get that spark or enjoyment out of things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your depression is mild, or is it interfering with your daily life more significantly?\n\nPatient: Well, I'd say it's definitely interfering. I mean... I've had some near-missed shifts lately. And I'm just having a lot more trouble keeping up with the POS reports and inventory at work. It really makes me worry that I could lose my job, and... well, then I'd lose my condo. I've already made some stupid mistakes with my mortgage paperwork recently because my head just isn't in it. \n\nPlus, I've really pulled back from my brother, and my coworkers too. I just keep to myself. It's gotten to the point where people have actually started noticing and saying that I'm not myself. So yeah, it's definitely affecting my daily life a lot.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by feeling like you've let your family down and that you deserve bad things to happen to you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's just... I took on this mortgage for the condo, and I keep feeling like I've completely let my family down by doing it. Like it was a mistake I can't fix. And then at work, every time I mess up an order or fall behind, it just feels like proof that I'm useless. I just get this overwhelming feeling that I deserve whatever bad things happen to me because of it. That's actually a big part of why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your depression has been present for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I would say so. For most of this whole period, even when some days were a little bit lighter, I've been weighed down by that empty, guilty feeling more often than not. It's just... it's pretty much always there in the background, most of the day, almost every day.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your depression has interfered with your ability to function at work or in other areas of your life for at least two years?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not two years. I'd say... these depressed, guilty periods have been present for about a year and two months of this whole time. So it hasn't been quite two years, but it's still been going on for a while.\n\nDoctor: Has your depression lasted for two years or more?\n\nPatient: No, like I just mentioned, it hasn't been two years. It's been about a year and two months.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Um, usually it happens when I'm closing up the restaurant for the night, or when I'm just sitting alone in my condo. I'll hear this low voice... it'll say my name, or sometimes it whispers that I'm going to lose everything. It sounds real, too. Real enough that I actually turn around to check if someone is standing right behind me.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices or visions happen only when you're feeling really down, or can they happen at any time?\n\nPatient: They can happen at other times, too. Like... there was a stretch of a few weeks a while back where my mood actually wasn't too bad. I wasn't feeling especially depressed, and I was getting through my shifts at the restaurant a lot easier. But even then, I still heard the whispering when I was alone at the condo. And I still had that feeling like I was being watched, especially when I was looking over my mortgage paperwork. So, yeah, it's not just when I'm feeling down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear are real, or do you know they are just a product of your own mind?\n\nPatient: I'm... I'm not really sure. In the moment, they sound completely real. Like I said, I actually turn around expecting someone to be standing right there. But then, you know, when the room is empty and I know I'm the only one in the condo... I guess logically I know it has to be my mind playing tricks on me. It's just hard to shake the feeling that it's real when it sounds so clear.\n\nDoctor: Has your depression lasted for two years or more?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn't been two years. I'd say the really low, depressed periods... the times where I just feel guilty and empty... that's been going on for about a year and two months now. That's when I first started noticing the mood changes during this whole thing.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices you hear typically occur when you're feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they do happen then, too. Um... during the worst depressed stretches, the whispering and that whole belief that the bank or my manager was watching me were still there. So I was basically dealing with both at the exact same time, just... trying to get through my shifts at the restaurant while feeling completely empty and hearing all that. It's a lot to handle all at once.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices you hear only happen when you're feeling depressed, or can they happen when your mood is not as low?\n\nPatient: No, they don't just happen when I'm depressed. There was actually a stretch of... I think a few weeks, where my mood wasn't really low at all. I was actually getting through my shifts at the restaurant a lot better. But even then, I still heard the whispering when I was at the condo. And I still felt like I was being watched, you know, with the whole mortgage paperwork thing. So, yeah, it still happens even when my mood is okay."
